### FR-FLOW-040 — tdd_policy default derives from work-mode with explicit-flag precedence

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.4.0 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | tdd-mode, g4-integration, skills |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/research/tddmode/06.g4-orchestrator-integration.md |

#### Requirement

The work-mode is the superior project-level declaration and the plan tdd_policy remains a plan-contract field whose DEFAULT derives from it. A shared mapping document (_shared/kiwi/workmode-policy.md, all three variants) MUST define the single-source mapping — tdd derives strict; sdd, wait, and vibe derive relaxed; disabled is never derived (explicit flag only) — and the explicit --tdd-policy flag always wins over the derived default with a non-fatal WARN. The kiwi-planner skill (three variants) MUST read the work-mode at Phase 0 (MCP get_work_mode first, CLI `speckiwi mode` fallback) and apply the derived default per the shared document. The kiwi-pm skill (three variants) MUST emit a non-HALT warning when the input plan tdd_policy contradicts the current work-mode (e.g., mode tdd with relaxed plan); the existing §7.1 disabled-HALT stays unchanged.

#### Rationale

G4 model A policy half (committee 2026-07-17 Q1, 5:0): without derivation, mode=tdd still produces relaxed plans — the two tdd systems stay contradictory. Mapping lives in one shared doc per the architecture condition (no logic duplication across skills); explicit-over-preset follows the FR-FLOW-034 --loops precedent.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: THE shared mapping document _shared/kiwi/workmode-policy.md SHALL exist in all three variants and define tdd→strict, sdd/wait/vibe→relaxed, disabled-never-derived, and explicit-over-derived precedence with WARN.
- [x] AC-2: THE kiwi-planner SKILL.md in all three variants SHALL cite the shared document, read the work-mode at Phase 0 (MCP-first, CLI fallback), and apply the derived tdd_policy default with the explicit flag winning.
- [x] AC-3: THE kiwi-pm SKILL.md in all three variants SHALL add a non-HALT work-mode/tdd_policy mismatch warning citing the shared document, and the §7.1 tdd_policy=disabled HALT SHALL stay unchanged.
- [x] AC-4: Content tests SHALL assert the derivation and mismatch-warning tokens across the three variants, and the existing planner/pm content suites stay green.

### FR-FLOW-041 — kiwi-tdd Phase 6 restores code traceability for promoted requirements

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.4.0 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | medium |
| Tags | tdd-mode, traceability, skills |
| Risk | low |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/research/tddmode/07.tdd-traceability-tags.md |

#### Requirement

The kiwi-tdd skill (all three variants: claude, codex, etc) MUST add a Phase 6 (post-promote) traceability step that restores, for the requirement promoted from the step, the same code traceability the sdd path produces: (1) an authoritative SRS Code anchor via add_trace_link(type=code, relation=implements, reference=<touched production file>) on the promoted body requirement, and (2) an auxiliary `@req <REQ-ID>` grep breadcrumb on the Phase 4 production code, reconciled from the vibe-style task-name tag to the final promoted Requirement ID per the FR-FLOW-020 convention. Both MUST be applied only after promote confirms the body Requirement ID (never at Phase 2/4), MUST be non-gating (a missing or stale code trace link or @req tag never blocks promote and is separate from the FR-NODE-074 EVIDENCE_REQUIRED gate), and the @req format/exemption rules are cited from kiwi-coder §0.17 (single-line format, test-file exemption, placement) WITHOUT importing kiwi-coder operational hooks. The Trace Links Code anchor is the authoritative obligation (traceability SSOT); the @req tag is the auxiliary breadcrumb. Phase 4 MUST record the touched production files so Phase 6 cites the correct paths.

#### Rationale

Investigation (docs/research/tddmode/07.tdd-traceability-tags.md): the tdd path writes production code (Phase 4) but promotes requirements with no code Trace Links and no @req tags, a traceability double-gap versus sdd. Committee 2026-07-18 Q1=A/Q2=Yes (5:0): reconcile at Phase 6 where promote confirms the byte-for-byte body ID (drift-free), reusing FR-FLOW-020's vibe task-name mechanism; Trace Links (SSOT) authoritative, @req auxiliary, both non-gating.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: THE kiwi-tdd SKILL.md in all three variants SHALL document a Phase 6 (post-promote) step that calls add_trace_link(type=code, relation=implements) on the promoted requirement citing the Phase 4 touched production file(s), and SHALL record touched production files during Phase 4.
- [x] AC-2: THE Phase 6 step SHALL reconcile the Phase 4 production-code trace tag from the vibe-style task-name to the final promoted Requirement ID as a `@req <REQ-ID>` breadcrumb, citing FR-FLOW-020 as the convention and kiwi-coder §0.17 for format/exemption only (no operational-hook import; test files exempt).
- [x] AC-3: THE skill SHALL state that both the code Trace Link and the @req tag are non-gating — a missing or stale tag/link never blocks promote and is separate from the EVIDENCE_REQUIRED gate — and that the Trace Links Code anchor is authoritative while @req is the auxiliary breadcrumb.
- [x] AC-4: THE traceability step SHALL be applied only after promote confirms the body Requirement ID (never at Phase 2/4), and a content test SHALL assert these tokens across the three variants while the existing kiwi-tdd content suite stays green.

### FR-FLOW-042 — kiwi-wave-master run-root preflight gate

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.4.2 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | feasibility:high, worktree, run-root |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/research/12.kiwi-wave-master-worktree-isolation-research.md |

#### Requirement

Before decomposing waves, kiwi-wave-master SHALL resolve the run root and verify that the SpecKiwi MCP workspace root reported by `mcp_workspace_info` matches the run's git root (`git rev-parse --show-toplevel`). The two paths SHALL be normalized before comparison — separators unified, a trailing separator dropped, case ignored on case-insensitive hosts including the drive letter, and symlinks resolved where possible — because on Windows git reports forward slashes while the MCP reports backslashes, and a raw string comparison would halt every legitimate run. On mismatch it SHALL halt immediately as a critical gate, before any SRS mutation, wave target registration, journal path resolution, or child pipeline spawn, and `--auto` SHALL NOT bypass that halt. Failure to obtain `workspaceRoot` from `mcp_workspace_info` SHALL be treated identically to a mismatch, and the `speckiwi` CLI SHALL NOT serve as a fallback source for this comparison, because the CLI resolves its root from the caller's cwd and would make the check vacuous. The halt output SHALL name both supported recovery paths — run the cycle in a single root by switching branches, or start a new agent session whose working directory is the intended worktree — SHALL warn that a session rooted at a diverged worktree must not allocate new Requirement IDs, and SHALL warn that such a session cannot edit the host repository's `docs/spec/`. kiwi-wave-master SHALL NOT delegate worktree isolation to `kiwi-pipeline --wt` and SHALL refuse such a request, because per-wave worktree creation breaks the wave-to-wave accumulation the orchestrator depends on, and SHALL offer the same two recovery paths instead. The epic-issue entry mode SHALL be covered by the same gate.

#### Rationale

Measured evidence: with the session cwd moved into a worktree, `mcp_workspace_info` still reported the host root (`C:\Work\git\_Snoworca\speckiwi`, rootSource=server-cwd-discovery) while the worktree's own SRS declared a different Active Target (v2.3.0.1 vs v2.4.1). MCP root is bound to the server process cwd by REL-MCP-004/IR-CLI-045 and the harness does not restart the server when the session directory changes, so a mismatch cannot be repaired mid-session. kiwi-wave-master v0.1 has no root vocabulary and no preflight, so the mismatch surfaces as silent writes into the wrong repository instead of an explicit stop.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: kiwi-wave-master resolves the run root and compares it with the MCP-reported workspaceRoot before any wave decomposition, target registration, SRS mutation, journal path resolution, or child pipeline spawn.
- [x] AC-2: The skill contract states that a mismatch is a critical halt which `--auto` does not bypass, and that at the halt point zero SRS mutations have been performed and no child skill has been spawned.
- [x] AC-3: The halt output names both recovery paths (single-root branch switch; new session rooted at the intended worktree), states the no-new-Requirement-ID caveat for a diverged worktree session, and warns that a worktree-rooted session cannot edit the host repository's `docs/spec/` because it lies outside that session's working directory.
- [x] AC-4: The epic-issue entry mode is explicitly covered by the same preflight gate.
- [x] AC-5: All three source skill variants (`skills/claude`, `skills/codex`, `skills/etc`) carry the gate contract and are asserted by content tests; the `.agents` mirror is out of scope because it intentionally excludes kiwi-wave-master per `.agents/skills/.speckiwi-mirror-exclusions.json`.
- [x] AC-6: Failing to obtain `workspaceRoot` from `mcp_workspace_info` is treated exactly like a mismatch — a critical halt — and the CLI fallback does not substitute for this gate.
- [x] AC-7: kiwi-wave-master does not delegate worktree isolation to `kiwi-pipeline --wt`; a `--wt` request is explicitly refused on the ground that per-wave worktree creation breaks wave accumulation, and the same two recovery paths named in the halt output are offered instead.
- [x] AC-8: Both roots are normalized before comparison — separators unified, trailing separator dropped, case ignored on case-insensitive hosts including the drive letter, symlinks resolved where possible — with a worked before/after example, so a Windows host where `git rev-parse` reports `C:/...` and `mcp_workspace_info` reports `C:\...` is judged a match instead of halting every legitimate run.

### FR-FLOW-043 — Run-root pinned wave and pipeline event journals

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.4.2 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | feasibility:high, worktree, run-root |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/research/12.kiwi-wave-master-worktree-isolation-research.md |

#### Requirement

The shared wave-event and pipeline-event contracts SHALL resolve `waves.jsonl` and `pipeline.jsonl` from a run root pinned once at run start instead of re-resolving the root on every emit, so a run cannot split its journal across two repositories mid-flight. The documented emit fallback chain SHALL also be correct: every POSIX-shell example's `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` failure path SHALL actually reach the cwd and home fallbacks, rather than producing a non-empty `/kiwi` path that makes every later emptiness check unreachable and can direct writes outside the repository. A run that halts at the FR-FLOW-042 preflight gate SHALL NOT pin a wave-journal root and SHALL NOT append to `waves.jsonl`, because no wave has started; its mandatory end-of-run `pipeline.jsonl` event SHALL still be emitted exactly once with `status=FAILED` and `next_hint=null`, under a `skill` value the pipeline-event schema accepts, resolving the path through the documented pipeline-event location chain evaluated once against the run's own cwd and never from the MCP-reported root, so that pipeline resume observes the halt.

#### Rationale

Two defects were measured. First, journal roots follow `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` while MCP SRS mutations follow the server process cwd, so a run started in one root and continued in another silently splits its progress log from its requirements; the host and the wave-master worktree already hold separate `kiwi/pipeline.jsonl` files. Second, the documented emit snippet writes `DIR=$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel 2>/dev/null)/kiwi`, which yields `/kiwi` when git fails, so the subsequent `[ -z "$DIR" ]` fallbacks can never fire; on this Windows/Git-Bash host `/` maps to the Git installation directory, so the failure path would attempt a write outside the repository.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: The wave-event and pipeline-event contracts state that the journal root is pinned at run start and is not re-resolved per emit.
- [x] AC-2: Every POSIX-shell emit example assigns the `<root>/kiwi` path only after `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` succeeds, so a git failure leaves the variable empty and the documented cwd and home fallbacks are reachable; the PowerShell examples that already guard on `$LASTEXITCODE` stay unchanged.
- [x] AC-3: The corrected fallback shape is applied to every shipped copy: `skills/{claude,codex,etc}/_shared/kiwi/waves-event.md`, the three matching `pipeline-event.md` files, and the two `.agents/skills/_shared/kiwi/` mirror files (8 files total).
- [x] AC-4: A content test asserts, across every shipped copy, both the run-start root-pinning statement and the corrected fallback shape, and fails on the unreachable-fallback form in its `$(...)`, quoted, and backtick variants.
- [x] AC-5: A run halted by the FR-FLOW-042 preflight gate starts no wave, so it pins no wave-journal root and appends nothing to `waves.jsonl`. The mandatory end-of-run pipeline event is preserved: the halted run emits exactly one `pipeline.jsonl` event with `status=FAILED` and `next_hint=null`, resolving the path through the pipeline-event location chain evaluated once against the run's own cwd and never from the MCP-reported root, so pipeline resume observes the halt.
- [x] AC-6: `kiwi-wave-master` is a valid `skill` enum member in every shipped `pipeline-event.md` copy (`skills/{claude,codex,etc}/_shared/kiwi/pipeline-event.md` plus the `.agents/skills/_shared/kiwi/` mirror, 4 files), so the AC-5 halt event is not discarded by the consumer's "skill enum 외 값 -> WARN + skip" rule; the AC-4 content test asserts that enum membership across all four copies. No new row is added to the `pipeline-event.md` producer table or the `kiwi-pipeline` consumer table: both already carry an `any` x `FAILED` row, and a skill-specific row would override the mandatory retry/skip/abort gate that `--auto` must not bypass.
- [x] AC-7: The AC-5 halt-emit contract is authored in all three `skills/{claude,codex,etc}/kiwi-wave-master/SKILL.md` variants (the preflight halt path in the `## 2.1 Preflight` section and the pipeline-emit clause) and is asserted by `test/skills/kiwi-wave-master-content.test.ts`, so AC-5 has a content-test anchor consistent with `Verification Method = test`.

### FR-FLOW-044 — kiwi-wave-master end-of-wave two-verifier cross-verification

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.4.3 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/research/13.kiwi-wave-master-wave-end-cross-verification-research.md |

#### Requirement

After a wave's per-wave `/kiwi-pipeline` reports TASK_DONE and before that wave is marked complete in `waves.jsonl`, `kiwi-wave-master` SHALL run an end-of-wave cross-verification step in which exactly two verifiers evaluate the same pinned wave-evidence bundle — spawned as two subagents where the host allows it, and rendered as two sequential verification axes on a single-worker host profile that forbids parallel fan-out, without weakening the count. The two verifiers SHALL be differentiated by stance rather than by evidence — verifier 1 evaluating wave SRS intent realization together with process conformance, verifier 2 evaluating artifact quality together with cross-wave regression risk — because splitting the evidence between them would make mutual verification structurally impossible. The intent verifier's denominator SHALL be sourced externally from the wave target's requirement list rather than chosen by the verifier, SHALL enumerate every requirement and acceptance criterion rather than a sample, and each realized row SHALL carry a resolvable evidence pointer. One round SHALL comprise three ordered steps — an isolated independent step, then a cross-refutation step in which each verifier may only add or confirm findings against the other's claims and never dismiss them, then a mechanical-union merge — and a round that skips the cross-refutation step SHALL be invalid, so that the isolated step alone can never be treated as a round. The step SHALL declare that per-hunk code review is out of scope because `kiwi-review-fix-loop` already owns it.

#### Rationale

No existing layer holds a wave's acceptance-criteria set against the finished artifact. planner coverage is decided before code exists and is persistent, coder verifies one Task against one Task spec, and review-fix-loop reads the diff under a gate meaning "no reviewer objected". A partially implemented criterion yields a diff that is defect-free over the part present, so the absent part is undetectable by a diff-scoped reviewer. A scope gap, not a rigor gap.


#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: All three shipped kiwi-wave-master SKILL.md variants place the end-of-wave cross-verification step after the per-wave pipeline reports TASK_DONE and before the waves.jsonl complete append, the phase-flow block lists it between Phase 3 and Phase 4, and the sentence that governs when the next wave starts gates on that verification rather than on TASK_DONE alone.
- [x] AC-2: The step runs exactly two verifiers — never at-least-two — and both receive the same wave-evidence bundle; the text states that giving them disjoint evidence is prohibited because it would make cross-verification structurally impossible. The etc variant renders the pair as two sequential verification axes because its single-worker host profile forbids parallel subagent fan-out, without weakening the count.
- [x] AC-3: The two verifiers are differentiated by stance: verifier 1 covers wave SRS intent realization plus process conformance and rolls up as ALL_MATCH or GAPS; verifier 2 covers artifact quality plus cross-wave regression risk and rolls up as substantive_clean.
- [x] AC-4: The wave-evidence bundle is enumerated and reaches the wave's own execution record, including the wave-scoped pipeline.jsonl window, the session worklog, the plan and sidecar, the review-fix-loop report, the wave target's requirement list, and the wave-window diff. The bundle is bounded by the wave's pipeline_run_id and its in_progress timestamp, and it states how each artifact is keyed: sessions and plans by the plan run-id, and the review-fix-loop report by that skill's own analysis dir rather than by the plan run-id or an unbounded glob.
- [x] AC-5: The intent verifier's denominator is obtained from the wave target's requirement list rather than chosen by the verifier, and the text says so in the negative; every requirement and acceptance criterion is enumerated as a row, checked must equal expected, and each row asserted as realized carries a resolvable evidence pointer; an unresolvable pointer or a count mismatch invalidates the round.
- [x] AC-6: Round 1 is independent and isolated, receiving neither the main session's conclusions nor the other verifier's output; rounds 2 and later spawn fresh verifiers that receive prior findings only as stripped claims and evidence pointers, without rationale, verdict, or confidence.
- [x] AC-7: The cross-refutation round is add-only: a verifier may add or confirm findings against the other's claims but may never dismiss them, and the merge is a mechanical union that leaves no discretion to either verifier or to the main session. The one sanctioned close path that removes a finding without a fix — unanimous reclassification — costs a further verification round regardless of mode, so it is not free in the default mode that has no streak.
- [x] AC-8: The step declares that per-hunk code review is out of scope because kiwi-review-fix-loop already owns it, so the layer evaluates wave-level evidence rather than re-reviewing the diff.

### FR-FLOW-045 — Wave verification convergence and delegated remediation

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.4.3 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/research/13.kiwi-wave-master-wave-end-cross-verification-research.md |

#### Requirement

The end-of-wave cross-verification step SHALL converge under a bounded loop whose passing condition is a round that applied no fixes, so a round that changed the artifact always forces a further verification round and a verdict is never recorded against a state the verifiers did not read. Under the default mode the passing condition SHALL be zero CRITICAL and zero HIGH merged findings together with a full intent roll-up; under `--max` it SHALL additionally require zero MEDIUM across consecutive passing rounds — two by default, and three where the host profile mandates three consecutive clean evaluations, as the single-worker `etc` profile does. Round caps SHALL follow the shared loop-option contract, defaulting to 5 and to 8 under `--max`, and cap exhaustion SHALL NOT be treated as a pass. Remediation SHALL be delegated rather than performed inside the orchestrator: quality, regression, and implementation findings go to `kiwi-review-fix-loop` with an explicit review scope, an intent gap for which no plan task ever existed escalates to re-entering that wave's `/kiwi-pipeline`, a finding that no code change can close is carried as residual for user decision, and a fix that would touch a previous wave halts. `kiwi-wave-master` SHALL NOT introduce its own fixer subagent, a fixer SHALL NOT edit acceptance-criteria text, weaken or delete an existing test, or author a finding's severity class, and a delegated fix loop reporting its own pass SHALL NOT by itself close a wave-level finding.

#### Rationale

-

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: All three shipped kiwi-wave-master SKILL.md variants state that the loop passes only on a round in which no fixes were applied, so any round that applied fixes is followed by a further verification round.
- [x] AC-2: The default passing condition is zero CRITICAL and zero HIGH merged findings plus a full intent roll-up. The --max passing condition additionally requires zero MEDIUM across consecutive passing rounds: two in the claude and codex variants, and three in the etc variant, whose single-worker profile mandates three consecutive clean evaluations and turns --max on by default; the etc variant cites that profile as the reason its streak differs.
- [x] AC-3: Round caps follow the shared loop-option contract: default 5, 8 under --max, 3 under --mini, and an explicit --loops N wins over --mini with a non-fatal warning.
- [x] AC-4: Cap exhaustion is never recorded as a pass: the run stops, records a cap-exhausted verdict with the complete residual finding list, does not append the wave complete event, and does not silently truncate residual findings.
- [x] AC-5: Remediation is delegated and routed by finding class: quality, regression, and implementation findings go to kiwi-review-fix-loop with an explicit review scope so that already-committed work is not reported clean from an empty working-tree diff; an intent gap with no corresponding plan task escalates to re-entering that wave's pipeline; a finding no code change can close is carried as residual for user decision.
- [x] AC-6: kiwi-wave-master does not introduce its own fixer subagent, and the text states that the orchestrator itself neither authors requirements nor implements code.
- [x] AC-7: A fixer may not edit acceptance-criteria text, may not weaken or delete an existing test, and may not author or alter a finding's severity class, which belongs to the verifier that raised it.
- [x] AC-8: A delegated fix loop reporting its own pass does not by itself close a wave-level finding; only re-verification by the two verifiers closes one.

The PASS rows, cap numbers, routing row and prohibitions are anchored to their own table rows because window-based checks were shown to survive deleting the row. 2026-07-29 full suite 211 files / 1484 passed / 0 failed. |

### FR-FLOW-046 — Wave verification record and kiwi-wave-master critical gate declaration

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.4.3 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/research/13.kiwi-wave-master-wave-end-cross-verification-research.md |

#### Requirement

The shared wave-event contract SHALL gain optional fields that make a wave verification outcome durable and auditable, as an additive minor version change that adds no member to the status enum, so the existing resume rule keyed on a latest status other than complete continues to express both a wave under verification and a wave whose verification failed. A complete event whose wave has no preceding passing wave-verification record in the same run SHALL be invalid and SHALL be treated by resume as incomplete, so that skipping the verification step entirely is detectable rather than producing a journal indistinguishable from a pre-feature run. That invalidation SHALL apply only to events at or above the bumped schema version, so complete records written before the feature keep resuming as done; because the version is written by the same producer the rule polices, the exemption SHALL be decided per run rather than per self-declared version, and a run containing any bumped-version event SHALL be covered regardless of what version an individual line claims. The residual finding list SHALL be complete when present, a round counter SHALL persist across resume while a passing verdict SHALL NOT, and `kiwi-wave-master` SHALL declare a `critical_gates[]` table, whose absence today means the shared auto-option contract treats its `--auto` as inactive and leaves the new gate no channel in which to be critical. The declaration SHALL transcribe the halts the skill body already mandates alongside the new verification halts, and residual CRITICAL or HIGH findings SHALL halt the whole wave orchestration even under `--auto`.

#### Rationale

-

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: All four shipped copies of the shared wave-event contract declare schema version 1.3.0 and add only optional fields, and the status enum keeps exactly its four existing members with the transition diagram unchanged; no added field is promoted into the required-field table, which would be a breaking change. The single bump from 1.0.0 covers this feature together with every later additive change up to that version, because no intermediate version was ever committed or shipped and no consumer ever observed one.
- [x] AC-2: The added optional fields record the verification phase and outcome: rounds used, the cap in force, a verdict distinguishing a non-terminal round from a pass, a residual failure, and cap exhaustion, each verifier's roll-up under the same token names the skill writes, the residual finding list, and the report path. The cap gloss matches the shared loop-option numbers.
- [x] AC-3: The contract states that a complete event whose wave has no latest passing wave-verification record in the same run is invalid and that resume treats such a wave as incomplete; that invalidation is scoped to the bumped version and later events so pre-existing complete records still resume as done, and the exemption is decided per run rather than per self-declared version so that emitting a downgraded version is not a bypass. Bare section pointers resolve within the contract, and every cross-document pointer names its document.
- [x] AC-4: The contract states that the residual finding list is complete when present and is never truncated, that an absent verification object on a complete event means unverified rather than clean, and that status remains the only positive completion signal while the verification record is a precondition that can invalidate a complete.
- [x] AC-5: The contract states that the round counter persists across resume while a passing verdict does not, and that a resumed orchestration reuses the same run identifier so the run-scoped gate and the resume-crossing counter cannot end up in different runs; the skill persists that counter once per round rather than once after the loop, so a crash mid-loop does not reset it.
- [x] AC-6: kiwi-wave-master records pipeline_run_id on the complete and wave-verify events, exempting the wave-start in_progress event whose pipeline cycle does not yet exist. The field opens that wave's pipeline.jsonl window, from which the plan run-id is resolved via the planner and pm events' artifacts fields; the review-fix-loop report resolves from that skill's own analysis dir in the same window, and an absent review-fix-loop event is raised as a finding rather than replaced by a glob.
- [x] AC-7: All three shipped kiwi-wave-master SKILL.md variants declare a critical_gates table with gate id, reason, and location columns that transcribes the run-root preflight mismatch, the refusal to delegate worktree isolation, and the child pipeline NEEDS_USER or FAILED halts, and adds a residual-critical verification gate and a cross-wave fix gate.
- [x] AC-8: The skill states that residual CRITICAL or HIGH findings, a gapped intent roll-up, or cap exhaustion halt the whole wave orchestration — not merely the current wave — even under --auto, and the change notes record that declaring critical_gates changes --auto from inactive to active for this skill.

### FR-FLOW-047 — kiwi-wave-master design baseline and decomposition coverage gate

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.4.4 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | critical |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/research/14.kiwi-wave-master-user-requirement-fit-evaluation.md |

#### Requirement

When kiwi-wave-master decomposes an input document into waves, it SHALL materialise a per-wave design baseline that pins each wave to a concrete source range, and SHALL NOT begin target registration until every top-level section of the input is either assigned to a wave or recorded as explicitly out of scope with a reason.

#### Rationale

Round-1 evaluation finding H4 and M3 and M10. Section 3 of the skill defines two split heuristics but no procedure that checks whether the split covers the input, and section 0.5 forbids re-splitting during execution, so a design section that lands in no wave is lost and cannot be reintroduced. The wave order also assumes each wave founds the next, yet the split never looks at the existing dependency direction.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: The skill SHALL accept an architecture design document or SDS as a first-class entry input, listed alongside research, plan, and roadmap documents.
- [x] AC-2: Wave decomposition SHALL emit a mapping of wave id to source file, heading path, and line range, and SHALL persist it as a design baseline artifact whose path is recorded on the wave's first waves.jsonl event.
- [x] AC-3: Decomposition SHALL compare the mapping against the input's top-level section set and SHALL report every unassigned section; a run with an unassigned section and no recorded out-of-scope reason SHALL NOT proceed to target registration.
- [x] AC-4: The wave-split subagent SHALL receive a summary of the existing module and dependency structure, and each wave SHALL record the existing modules it is expected to touch.
- [x] AC-5: Appending a new wave to close a discovered design coverage gap SHALL be an explicit exception to the wave-boundary immutability rule, and SHALL NOT be treated as re-decomposition; the order of already registered waves SHALL remain unchanged.
- [x] AC-6: The design baseline artifact SHALL be resolvable from waves.jsonl alone, without relying on conversation state.

Hedge vocabulary is asserted absent from each MUST sentence. |
| VE-2 | test | test/skills/kiwi-event-contract-content.test.ts | AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-6 | describe "FR-FLOW-047/048/049/051 — waves-event v1.2.0 continuity fields" asserts over all four shipped waves-event.md copies (skills/{claude,codex,etc} + .agents mirror) that the design_baseline object declares path/source_file/heading_path/line_start/line_end rows (AC-2), out_of_scope with both heading and reason plus existing_modules rows (AC-3/AC-4), and states the artifact is resolvable from waves.jsonl alone (AC-6); design_baseline stays an optional field rather than being promoted to required. |

### FR-FLOW-048 — Wave-verification denominator includes the design baseline and declared constraints

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.4.4 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | critical |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/research/14.kiwi-wave-master-user-requirement-fit-evaluation.md |

#### Requirement

When the end-of-wave cross-verification runs, the evidence bundle SHALL include that wave's design baseline range and the declared user constraints, and verifier 1 SHALL enumerate design items as a second denominator layer such that an unmapped design item forbids an ALL_MATCH roll-up.

#### Rationale

Round-1 evaluation finding C1, M1 and M9. The bundle's seven rows contain no design or research input and the denominator is fixed to the wave target's requirements, so a design item that never became a requirement cannot appear in the denominator and the wave passes by definition. Verifier 2, the only stance that looks at damage to existing structure, has no external denominator at all.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: The evidence bundle SHALL include a required row for the wave's design baseline range and a required row for the declared user constraints, resolved from the artifacts recorded on waves.jsonl rather than from conversation state.
- [x] AC-2: Verifier 1 SHALL enumerate every design item in that wave's baseline range as a row and record for each the requirement id it maps to, or mark it unmapped.
- [x] AC-3: A roll-up of ALL_MATCH SHALL be forbidden when at least one design item is unmapped, independently of the requirement and acceptance-criteria layer being complete.
- [x] AC-4: The verification object SHALL carry the design layer counts and the unmapped design items, and the waves-event contract SHALL define those fields.
- [x] AC-5: Verifier 2 SHALL receive a mechanically derived denominator from the wave diff consisting of removed or moved existing files, removed or changed existing public symbols, and removed or modified existing test files, and SHALL enumerate each as a row judged as intended improvement or unapproved damage.
- [x] AC-6: A round whose enumerated row count does not match the fixed denominator count SHALL be invalid and SHALL NOT count toward a consecutive clean streak, for both verifiers.

### FR-FLOW-049 — All-wave final verification against the whole design baseline

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.4.4 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/research/14.kiwi-wave-master-user-requirement-fit-evaluation.md |

#### Requirement

When the last wave has been marked complete, kiwi-wave-master SHALL run one final verification pass whose denominator is the whole design baseline plus the cross-wave integration items, and SHALL NOT report the orchestration as complete until that pass records a passing verdict.

#### Rationale

-

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: A final verification phase SHALL run after the last wave's complete event and before the orchestration is reported as finished.
- [x] AC-2: The final pass denominator SHALL be the whole design baseline mapped against the union of all wave requirements, plus the integration items that cross wave boundaries and therefore belong to no single wave scope.
- [x] AC-3: The final pass SHALL reuse the two-verifier stance separation, the add-only cross-refutation step, and the fix-free clean round condition defined for the per-wave loop.
- [x] AC-4: The final pass result SHALL be appended to waves.jsonl as an event scoped to the whole run rather than to a single wave, carrying its own verification object.
- [x] AC-5: The waves-event resume contract SHALL define overall completion as all waves complete and the final verification verdict passing, so that a run whose final pass has not passed resumes into the final pass rather than reporting completion.
- [x] AC-6: Residual findings from the final pass SHALL be reported in full and SHALL NOT be truncated to a sample or a top-N list.

### FR-FLOW-050 — Design-to-SRS fidelity wiring and the SRS-layer gap remediation path

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.4.4 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/research/14.kiwi-wave-master-user-requirement-fit-evaluation.md |

#### Requirement

When kiwi-wave-master registers a wave target, it SHALL pass that wave's materialised design baseline to kiwi-srs as a research document path so that the research verify-and-improve loop runs, and the end-of-wave remediation routing SHALL contain an entry that closes items present in the design but absent from the wave SRS.

#### Rationale

-

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: The per-wave kiwi-srs invocation SHALL pass the wave's design baseline as a research document path argument, because inlining the wave content alone does not activate the research verify-and-improve loop.
- [x] AC-2: The design baseline path passed to kiwi-srs SHALL be the same artifact path recorded on waves.jsonl, so the evidence bundle and the authoring input cannot diverge.
- [x] AC-3: The remediation routing table SHALL contain a row for findings that are present in the design baseline but absent from the wave SRS, and its handling SHALL be incremental SRS authoring re-entry for that wave target followed by pipeline re-entry from the planning stage.
- [x] AC-4: That row SHALL be distinct from the row for findings that cannot be closed in code, which remains a residual and a user decision, so that a design-layer gap is remediated rather than only reported.
- [x] AC-5: When the incremental authoring would add a requirement that touches a previously completed wave target, the existing cross-wave halt gate SHALL still apply.
- [x] AC-6: Automatic remediation of a design-layer gap SHALL be permitted under unattended operation, because its basis is the recorded design baseline rather than an inference.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/skills/kiwi-wave-continuity-content.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5, AC-6 | describe "FR-FLOW-050" asserts over all three kiwi-wave-master copies: §4 passes the wave's design_baseline.path to the kiwi-srs call as a research document path and states inlining alone does not activate the research loop (AC-1); that path is required to be the same one recorded on waves.jsonl (AC-2); the §5.5.5 remediation routing table has a design-present / SRS-absent row whose handling is incremental kiwi-srs re-entry followed by pipeline re-entry from planning (AC-3); that row is a separate table row from the unclosable-in-code residual + user-decision row (AC-4); the cross-wave halt stays in force for the remediation path (AC-5); automatic design-gap remediation is permitted under --auto (AC-6). |

### FR-FLOW-051 — Wave target registration and wave-internal resume granularity

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.4.4 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | critical |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/research/14.kiwi-wave-master-user-requirement-fit-evaluation.md |

#### Requirement

When a wave target is not present in the Target Map, the authoring skill SHALL register it with a creation-enabled activation call rather than failing, and waves.jsonl SHALL record enough state that resuming a partially processed wave does not re-author its SRS.

#### Rationale

-

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: The requirement authoring skill's target resolution SHALL define a step that registers an unregistered target through a creation-enabled activation call, and SHALL halt only when that registration itself fails.
- [x] AC-2: The command-line fallback table SHALL show the creation option for the target activation command, so that the fallback path can register a target as well.
- [x] AC-3: kiwi-wave-master SHALL state that registering an unregistered wave target with creation enabled is the normal path of that phase rather than an exceptional one.
- [x] AC-4: The waves-event contract SHALL define a record that marks a wave's SRS authoring as finished, distinguishable from the wave being merely in progress.
- [x] AC-5: kiwi-wave-master SHALL skip the target registration and SRS authoring phase for a wave that carries the authoring-finished mark on any of its events, not merely on its latest one, and SHALL enter the pipeline phase directly. Keying the decision to the latest event alone would re-author the SRS whenever a crash left a later verification event on top, which is the exact duplication this rule exists to prevent.
- [x] AC-6: The skill SHALL define wave-internal resume granularity as the three stages of target registration, pipeline, and wave verification, so that resume is not defined only at whole-wave granularity.

#### Verification Evidence

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |

| VE-2 | test | test/skills/kiwi-event-contract-content.test.ts | AC-4, AC-5 | "%s defines an SRS-authoring-finished record" asserts over all four waves-event.md copies that srs_authored is declared as an optional boolean field, that the finished mark rides on the phase="srs-authoring" record, and that the contract states it is distinguishable from authoring merely in progress; "%s extends the phase enum to four members" pins srs-authoring in the phase enum cell. Both are the contract half of AC-4 and the state AC-5's skip reads. |

### FR-FLOW-052 — Unattended-run continuity across the wave cycle entry contract and cost gates

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.4.4 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | critical |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/research/14.kiwi-wave-master-user-requirement-fit-evaluation.md |

#### Requirement

When kiwi-wave-master runs unattended, the cycle entry contract SHALL be unambiguous about spawning, gates that exist only to confirm cost or scheduling SHALL be reachable through explicit pass-through options, and gates that protect against damage SHALL remain in force.

#### Rationale

-

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: The cycle entry SHALL be unambiguous about whether the child pipeline spawns its stages, either by the orchestrator passing the run option explicitly or by the pipeline contract stating that cycle mode implies it.
- [x] AC-2: The multi-candidate ambiguity gate SHALL be declared as not applying to cycle-mode entry, because in cycle mode the next stage is fixed by the chain rather than chosen.
- [x] AC-3: kiwi-wave-master SHALL read the work mode during preflight and SHALL state that wave cycles are body-scope work, so that step-scoped routing to the test-first skill does not apply to a wave cycle.
- [x] AC-4: The cost-confirmation and integration-consent gates of the implementation skill SHALL be reachable from kiwi-wave-master through explicitly listed pass-through options, and the propagation path through the intermediate skills SHALL be stated.
- [x] AC-5: The integration-test consent gate SHALL carry the same child-context guard that the follow-on review gate already carries, so that a single-task child does not raise it on every task.
- [x] AC-6: kiwi-wave-master SHALL refuse the regression-skip and reviewer-off options in the same way it refuses worktree delegation, and SHALL state that the automatic propagation of the unattended flag to per-wave child skills follows the shared option contract.

### FR-FLOW-053 — Per-requirement partial progress instead of whole-run blocking

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.4.4 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/research/14.kiwi-wave-master-user-requirement-fit-evaluation.md |

#### Requirement

When a single requirement is blocked by its stability, by a failed task, or by a scope-boundary conflict, the affected skill SHALL confine the stop to that requirement and continue with the rest, recording the skipped item so the wave verification surfaces it as residual.

#### Rationale

-

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Under unattended operation, a requirement blocked by draft stability SHALL be handled by skipping only the tasks tracing to that requirement and reporting the skipped list, rather than halting the whole run.
- [x] AC-2: The end-of-wave remediation routing SHALL contain an entry for requirements left at draft by the feasibility or planning stages, so that such requirements have a defined destination instead of being unmapped.
- [x] AC-3: The task-failure escalation halt SHALL be declared in the critical gate table of the skill that raises it, so that the set of stopping points is predictable from the declared gates alone.
- [x] AC-4: When a predecessor task was skipped rather than failed, the dependency check SHALL be resolvable by an automated decision to continue or to skip the dependent task in turn, instead of an unconditional halt.
- [x] AC-5: Under unattended operation, a requirement whose authoring conflicts with a scope boundary SHALL be deferred and recorded rather than blocking authoring of the remaining requirements, and the whole-run block SHALL be reserved for an explicit user refusal.
- [x] AC-6: Every requirement skipped or deferred by these paths SHALL be recorded so that the end-of-wave verification reports it as residual rather than silently omitting it.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/skills/kiwi-wave-continuity-content.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5, AC-6 | describe "FR-FLOW-053" asserts over all three copies of kiwi-pm, kiwi-srs and kiwi-wave-master: kiwi-pm §4.3 skips only the tasks tracing to a draft requirement under --auto and reports the skipped list, and lifecycle-gate-draft is asserted absent from the unconditional critical gate table (AC-1); the kiwi-wave-master §5.5.5 remediation table routes requirements left at draft to per-REQ feasibility re-run and planning re-entry (AC-2); the kiwi-pm §0.G7 table declares task-failure-escalation as a gate row (AC-3); kiwi-pm §3.1.1 resolves an all-skipped predecessor set by automated decision to continue or skip, with the failed/blocked halt explicitly unchanged (AC-4); kiwi-srs §6.4 defers a scope-boundary-conflicting requirement under --auto, continues authoring the rest, and reserves the whole-run block for an explicit user refusal (AC-5); every skipped or deferred requirement surfaces as wave residual with a reason_class (AC-6). |
| VE-2 | test | test/skills/kiwi-event-contract-content.test.ts | AC-6 | "%s types the residual entries" asserts over all four waves-event.md copies that residual entries carry a reason_class whose enum defines draft-stability-skip, task-failure-skip and scope-boundary-deferred (the three destinations AC-1/AC-4/AC-5 produce) alongside srs-level-unclosable, design-gap and cross-wave-carry-forward, so a skipped or deferred requirement is typed rather than an untyped free-text blob. |

### FR-FLOW-054 — Existing-structure preservation guards across the implementation chain

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.4.4 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | critical |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/research/14.kiwi-wave-master-user-requirement-fit-evaluation.md |

#### Requirement

When the implementation chain changes an existing codebase, it SHALL treat existing tests as inviolable, SHALL judge regressions against a captured baseline, SHALL detect changes to existing public contracts from the diff, and SHALL confirm a green full suite before a wave is marked complete.

#### Rationale

-

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: The implementation skill SHALL declare that existing tests are inviolable, forbidding deletion of existing test files, removal of existing test cases, and weakening of existing assertions in order to reach a passing state.
- [x] AC-2: The plan-versus-code gate SHALL detect deletion of existing test files, removal of existing test cases, and weakening of existing assertions in the diff, and SHALL treat a detection as a critical finding that forces re-implementation.
- [x] AC-3: That detection SHALL be declared as a critical gate so that it halts even under unattended operation and cannot be resolved by an automated decision.
- [x] AC-4: The implementation skill SHALL capture a baseline suite result before it starts changing code, and SHALL judge regression by the delta against that baseline so that pre-existing failures are reported rather than attributed to the current task.
- [x] AC-5: The chain SHALL detect deletion of, or signature change to, existing public symbols from the diff and SHALL raise it as a distinct gate that is critical regardless of the file path, rather than relying on a path-token heuristic.
- [x] AC-6: The end-of-wave evidence bundle SHALL include a full regression suite run executed at that wave's head, recording the command, the exit code, the failing tests, and the baseline failing tests pinned for the run; a passing verdict SHALL require zero new failures, that is, the failing tests at the wave head contained in that baseline set, and SHALL be reduced to requiring a zero exit code only when the baseline could not be captured.

#### Verification Evidence

AC-6's pass condition was amended by FR-FLOW-057 AC-6 to the baseline delta; the row now also carries baseline_failing_tests and the §5.5.4 Normal row requires zero new failures with exit_code=0 kept only as the capture-failure fallback, and the assertions pinning that amended form live in kiwi-wave-continuity-r2-content.test.ts describe R2-H5. This file's AC-6 test still passes because the Normal row retains the exit_code=0 token inside that fallback clause, so it no longer pins the primary condition on its own. |

### FR-FLOW-055 — Cross-wave improvement carry-forward, decision rule, and re-entry scope

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.4.4 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/research/14.kiwi-wave-master-user-requirement-fit-evaluation.md |

#### Requirement

When a finding requires touching work that a completed wave produced, the skill SHALL decide cross-wave status by a mechanical rule, SHALL carry the work forward into a later wave rather than halting whenever that is possible, and SHALL bound the scope of any pipeline re-entry.

#### Rationale

-

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Cross-wave status SHALL be decided by a mechanical rule over the file set, namely intersection with the code trace anchors of the earlier wave's recorded requirements or with the earlier wave's diff file set, rather than by narrative judgement.
- [x] AC-2: A finding that requires changing an earlier wave's requirements SHALL remain a halt, because that would reverse a completed target.
- [x] AC-3: A finding that requires changing code an earlier wave produced SHALL be carried forward into the scope of a remaining wave, or into a newly appended wave when none remains, and SHALL be recorded as residual with a cross-wave marker.
- [x] AC-4: Carrying a finding forward SHALL NOT modify or reverse the earlier wave's complete event, so the append-only journal and the wave-boundary immutability rule are preserved.
- [x] AC-5: The halt SHALL be reserved for the case where neither carry-forward path is available, rather than being the first response to any cross-wave finding.
- [x] AC-6: Pipeline re-entry driven by a verification gap SHALL carry an explicit scope, namely the unaddressed requirement filter and a statement of whether the existing plan run identifier is reused, at the same level of specificity the review delegation row already requires.

### FR-FLOW-056 — Run-scoped wave journal computation and phase-accurate resume

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.4.4 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | critical |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/analysis/wave-fit-eval/round2-findings.md |

#### Requirement

When kiwi-wave-master computes wave status, resume position, or overall completion from waves.jsonl, it SHALL restrict every such computation to the events of one explicitly selected run_id, and SHALL derive each phase marker from the whole event history of that run rather than from its latest event alone.

#### Rationale

Round-2 evaluation findings C1, H9, M6, M11, M12, L4. The journal is one append-only file per repository and a wave is identified only as wave-{n}, yet the resume section computes each wave's state as the latest event for that wave with the sole filter being the exclusion of run-scope events, while the completion gate and the schema-version exemption in the same document are both explicitly scoped to a run. A second epic started in the same repository therefore reads the first epic's wave-1 complete and its final-verify pass as its own state and reports the whole orchestration finished with zero implementation; this holds silently under --auto and from the second use of the skill onward. The same latest-event reading governs the SRS authoring marker, so a crash inside the verification loop leaves a wave-verify event on top, the wave reads as still authoring, and the requirements are authored twice — the exact duplication the clause claims to prevent, and a contradiction with the journal contract, which asks only that such a record exist. Alongside this, the first in_progress event is labelled as the pipeline phase although authoring runs before the pipeline, the run-scope final-verify event carries no wave-verify record of its own so the completion gate reads it as invalid by construction, and no status value is defined for a final-verify event that does not pass.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Per-wave latest-status computation and the whole-run completion predicate SHALL consider only events whose run_id equals the selected run; events carrying any other run_id SHALL NOT contribute to either.
- [x] AC-2: The rule for selecting which run_id to resume SHALL be stated — an explicit argument when supplied, otherwise the most recent run that is not complete — and SHALL be stated in both the journal contract and the orchestrator skill.
- [x] AC-3: A wave's SRS authoring SHALL be treated as complete when any event of that wave within the selected run carries the authored marker, and the reading that an unmarked line means authoring is in progress SHALL apply only to events in the SRS-authoring phase.
- [x] AC-4: The first in_progress event of a wave SHALL carry the phase of the stage actually entered, so a wave whose SRS authoring precedes its pipeline records the SRS-authoring phase rather than the pipeline phase.
- [x] AC-5: The wave-verify precondition that a complete event depends on SHALL state how it applies to the run-scope final-verification event, so that a run-scope record is not invalid by construction.
- [x] AC-6: The status value carried by a final-verification event whose verdict is not pass SHALL be defined, and a run in that state SHALL NOT be reported as complete.
- [x] AC-7: When a run resumes with cumulative round counters while the clean streak restarts from zero, the termination conditions SHALL still admit a reachable pass outcome for that run.

### FR-FLOW-057 — Preservation and regression termination wiring with stated judgement criteria

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.4.4 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | critical |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/analysis/wave-fit-eval/round2-findings.md |

#### Requirement

When a wave verification round records a preservation judgement or a regression result, the wave termination conditions SHALL treat an unapproved-damage row as blocking and SHALL judge regression as a delta against a pinned baseline, and every such judgement SHALL rest on a stated detection criterion rather than on verifier discretion.

#### Rationale

Round-2 evaluation findings C2, H5, M2, M3, M14, M15, M16. The preservation layer is defined as a schema object and as a verifier denominator, but it appears in no termination condition: the wave pass table lists merged severity, the requirement-layer roll-up, the no-fix-applied clause and the regression exit code, and the journal contract's enforcement bullets name the unmapped design item and the regression exit code — neither names unapproved damage. A verifier that honestly records the deletion of three existing public APIs as unapproved damage therefore still satisfies the pass condition, a complete event is appended, and because that event cannot be reversed the breakage becomes permanent; the final pass reuses the same conditions and inherits the same hole. Where the layer does bind, the two-value verdict is left entirely to the verifier, and the terms the verdict turns on — existing public symbol, existing, weakened — are asserted as instructions with no detection rule, in contrast to the non-blocking requirement tag for which three regular expressions are supplied. Deletion and relocation of non-test files is not among the detection items of the plan-versus-code gate at all, although the planner treats file operations as a first-class task kind. In the opposite direction, the wave layer demands an absolute zero exit code while the implementation layer explicitly declares a non-zero baseline not to be a blocking condition and refuses the option that would skip regression, so in any existing codebase with one pre-existing failing test no wave can ever reach completion. The review-and-fix loop, the only code-change path that does not run through the implementation skill, carries neither the preservation prohibitions nor the baseline-delta rule.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: The wave termination conditions in both the Normal and the reinforced mode SHALL require zero unapproved-damage rows, and the all-wave final verification SHALL apply the same condition.
- [x] AC-2: The journal contract SHALL state the unapproved-damage blocking rule in the same enforcement form it uses for the unmapped design item, so that a single such row forbids recording a passing verdict.
- [x] AC-3: The decision rule that separates an intended improvement from unapproved damage SHALL be stated, so the verdict follows from recorded evidence rather than from verifier discretion.
- [x] AC-4: Detectable criteria SHALL be stated for the terms the preservation gates turn on — existing public symbol, existing artefact, and weakened test — at the same level of mechanical specificity as the existing requirement-tag detection rules.
- [x] AC-5: Deletion or relocation of an existing non-test file SHALL be a detection item of the plan-versus-code gate, and its presence in the plan sidecar file list alone SHALL NOT clear it.
- [x] AC-6: The wave-layer regression pass condition SHALL be that the failing tests at the wave head are contained in a run-pinned baseline set, with the baseline captured once during preflight and shared as one source of truth with the implementation skill's baseline; the absolute zero-exit-code condition SHALL apply only when baseline capture fails.
- [x] AC-7: The review-and-fix loop SHALL carry the same preservation prohibitions and a declared gate identifier for them, citing the implementation skill's preservation clause as their basis.
- [x] AC-8: The review-and-fix loop SHALL judge regression by the same baseline delta as the implementation skill, so the same pipeline does not tolerate a pre-existing failure at one stage and block on it at another.

#### Verification Evidence

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/skills/kiwi-wave-continuity-r2-content.test.ts | AC-1, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5, AC-6, AC-7, AC-8 | describes R2-C2, R2-H5, R2-M2, R2-M14, R2-M3 and R2-M16 assert over all three variants of kiwi-wave-master, kiwi-coder and kiwi-review-fix-loop: R2-C2 pins the §5.5.4 Normal row to carry "unapproved-damage 0 건" and pins the same condition in §5.6, unhedged (AC-1); R2-M16 pins the §5.5.2 rule that intended-improvement may be written only when a REQ or plan Task authorises it and that the authority is recorded in the row's evidence field, with an unapproved-damage default for rows that cannot cite one (AC-3); R2-M2 pins kiwi-coder §0.20.1/§0.20.2/§0.20.3 — baseline-commit definition of "existing", export-surface definition of "public symbol" and the four-item closed list defining "weakening" (AC-4); R2-M14 pins the §5.1.(d) detection of non-test file deletion or relocation and the §0.G6 row stating that sidecar file-list membership alone does not clear it (AC-5); R2-H5 pins the failing_tests subset-of-baseline_failing_tests pass condition, the §2.1 preflight one-shot run-pinned capture sharing one SSOT with kiwi-coder state.regression_baseline, and the exit_code=0 fallback restricted to capture failure (AC-6); R2-M3 pins kiwi-review-fix-loop §0.17 preservation prohibitions citing kiwi-coder §0.20 with its three §0.G8 gate rows, and its baseline capture / delta judgement / no-attribution rules (AC-7, AC-8). |

### FR-FLOW-058 — Mechanised design-layer and constraint-layer denominators for wave verification

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.4.4 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | critical |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/analysis/wave-fit-eval/round2-findings.md |

#### Requirement

When kiwi-wave-master materialises a wave's design baseline, it SHALL fix the design-layer denominator as an enumerated item list produced outside the verification round, SHALL deliver the design prose and the declared user constraints to requirement authoring, and SHALL make the declared constraints a verification layer with its own fixed denominator and blocking rule.

#### Rationale

Round-2 evaluation findings C3, H8, H10, M10. The cross-verification section requires every design item in the wave's baseline range to be enumerated as a row, but nothing defines what a design item is: the baseline materialises only a coordinate mapping and the journal schema carries no item array, so the expected count is computed by whichever verifier is spawned that round. The section's own stated principle — that a denominator is fixed from outside and never chosen by the verifier — is honoured for the requirement layer, which reads from the requirement store, and for the preservation layer, which derives mechanically from the diff, but not for the design layer; a round declaring twenty-one items and a round declaring six are equally valid, and the row-count invalidation rule presupposes a fixed denominator that does not exist, so the shortest path to zero unmapped items is to describe the design in coarser units. The authoring side has the mirror-image defect: the path handed to requirement authoring points at the coordinate mapping while the evidence bundle receives the prose range, so authoring compares requirements against a pointer and verification compares them against text, and the resulting design-layer gap recurs every wave, turning incremental re-authoring from an exception into the standing path that consumes the round cap. Declared user constraints are recorded only if constraints happen to exist, with no collection step, no place in the option mapping, no layer, and no rule making a violation fail any roll-up — yet the evidence bundle declares the constraint row mandatory and non-omittable while its source field is optional. Finally the decomposition coverage gate inspects only the top-level section layer, so design that lives in a nested section can go unassigned.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Design baseline materialisation SHALL emit an enumerated design item list in which each item carries an identifier, its heading path, its source line range, and its statement, and the rule defining what counts as one item SHALL be stated.
- [x] AC-2: The design layer's expected count SHALL be the length of that wave's enumerated item list, SHALL NOT be computed by the verifier, and the round-invalidating row-count comparison SHALL be made against that externally fixed count; the all-wave final pass SHALL derive its expected count the same way.
- [x] AC-3: Design baseline materialisation SHALL also produce a per-wave excerpt of the design prose as an artifact, and the requirement-authoring call SHALL receive that prose artifact as its research document rather than the coordinate mapping alone.
- [x] AC-4: The design baseline record SHALL point at the prose excerpt artifact, and the authoring input and the verification evidence bundle SHALL resolve to the same artifact.
- [x] AC-5: The decomposition coverage gate SHALL cover nested sections of the input document, not only its top-level section set.
- [x] AC-6: The orchestration SHALL include an explicit step that collects declared user constraints, and SHALL produce a constraints artifact on every run — an empty list when none were declared — so that the constraints path is always recorded on the journal.
- [x] AC-7: The verification record SHALL carry a constraint layer with its expected, checked, and violation counts, and one or more violations SHALL forbid the requirement-layer roll-up from being recorded as a full match.
- [x] AC-8: The requirement-authoring call SHALL receive the constraints artifact, a constraint raised after the run has started SHALL be appended as a new event, and resolution SHALL read the most recent constraints path.

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/skills/kiwi-wave-continuity-r2-content.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5, AC-6, AC-7, AC-8 | describes R2-C3, R2-H8 and R2-H10 assert over all three kiwi-wave-master variants: R2-C3 pins the §3.1 design_items materialisation carrying id, heading_path, line_start, line_end and statement, pins the item-unit rule ("최하위 헤딩 아래 규범 문장 1건 = 1 항목", examples excluded) unhedged, pins §5.5.2 fixing design_layer.expected to the design_items length with "검증자가 스스로 산정하지 않는다", pins the §5.5.2 row-count invalidation against the frozen denominator, pins §5.6 deriving the final denominator from all waves' design_items, and pins the §3.2 coverage-gate comparison unit as design_items rather than the top-level section layer (AC-1, AC-2, AC-5); R2-H8 pins the per-wave design-baseline/wave-{n}.md excerpt with excerpt_path recorded, and pins the §4 authoring call passing that excerpt as --research-doc while naming the coordinate JSON it replaces (AC-3, AC-4); R2-H10 pins the always-written constraints artifact including the empty-array case unhedged, the §5.5.2 constraint_layer with expected/checked/violations and the single-violation ALL_MATCH block, the §4 hand-off of constraints_path to authoring, and the late-constraint rule appending a new in_progress event with resolution reading the latest constraints_path (AC-6, AC-7, AC-8). |

### FR-FLOW-059 — Unattended wave-run continuity across undeclared stops and evidence windows

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.4.4 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/analysis/wave-fit-eval/round2-findings.md |

#### Requirement

When kiwi-wave-master runs unattended, every point at which the run can stop, lose its evidence window, or lose its denominator SHALL be resolved by the run's own declared options or declared as a gate row, and no such point SHALL depend on a value the user must type.

#### Rationale

Round-2 evaluation findings H1, H3, M4, M5, M7, M8, L1, L2, L3. The implementation skill keeps two consent gates that unattended mode alone cannot resolve — integration-test consent and the large-task cost warning — because both are declared resolvable only on explicit input, and the pass-through chain that would carry them exists but has no source: the natural-language option table has no row for either, so their values exist only if the user types them. Since long tasks and integration tests are near-default in a coding wave, the run stops in its first wave, at a stop that no gate table predicts. The same undeclared class covers the requirement-authoring child, which the orchestrator calls directly rather than through the pipeline, so its own critical gates return through a path the orchestrator's pipeline-scoped gate row does not name; the gate section's own prose count and positional references no longer match its table. The document-selection question at the start of decomposition is likewise an undeclared gate, which the shared unattended contract resolves by committee default. Beyond stopping, the run loses information it needs: the reinforced mode does not reach the per-wave authoring call because propagation is described only for what the pipeline cycle spawns, the evidence window is a single pipeline run identifier while every re-entry mints a new one, so verification reads either pre-fix evidence or stale clean evidence, and no point freezes the verification denominators, so authoring and fixes inside the loop keep enlarging them and invalidated rounds consume the cap. A crash leaves the task-runner lock in place with no propagation path to clear it on resume, and the terminal hop of the cycle chain is undefined against the transition table, leaving a possible unannounced external side effect.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: The integration-test consent gate and the large-task cost-warning gate SHALL be reachable through the orchestrator's declared pass-through option chain, the orchestrator SHALL state that its unattended option alone does not pass them and that each is bypassed only when its own option is explicitly supplied, and both gate identifiers SHALL appear in the orchestrator's gate table.
- [x] AC-2: A directly invoked requirement-authoring child that returns a needs-user or failed outcome SHALL be a declared gate row covering both the per-wave authoring step and the verification-driven re-authoring step, and the surrounding prose SHALL name that child alongside the pipeline child.
- [x] AC-3: Every count and positional reference in the gate section's explanatory prose SHALL agree with the rows of the gate table it describes.
- [x] AC-4: The question asking which document to decompose SHALL be a declared gate rather than an undeclared decision resolved by the shared unattended contract's default.
- [x] AC-5: The reinforced mode SHALL propagate to the per-wave requirement-authoring call that the orchestrator invokes directly, in the same way the round-cap options already do.
- [x] AC-6: A stale task-runner lock left by a crashed run SHALL be resolvable on resume through a declared propagation path, so that pipeline resume is not blocked by it.
- [x] AC-7: The verification evidence window SHALL resolve every pipeline run belonging to that wave, including runs created by re-entry, so a round never judges against pre-fix evidence or against a clean result predating the latest fix.
- [x] AC-8: The verification denominators SHALL have a declared freeze point per round, so authoring or fixes applied during the loop do not retroactively invalidate rounds and consume the round cap.
- [x] AC-9: The terminal hop of the cycle chain SHALL be unambiguous against the transition table, and any external side effect at that hop SHALL be a declared gate.

### FR-FLOW-060 — Executable remediation routing for wave verification findings

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.4.4 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/analysis/wave-fit-eval/round2-findings.md |

#### Requirement

When a wave or final verification round produces a finding that requires a fix, the run SHALL have an executable route from that finding to a scoped re-entry that actually changes code, and every input that route depends on SHALL be resolvable from the journal alone.

#### Rationale

Round-2 evaluation findings H2, H4, H7, M1, M13. The verification-driven re-entry is required to pass an unaddressed-requirement filter and a decision about reusing the plan run identity, but the child it re-enters accepts neither: its option table has no requirement filter and no plan run argument, and the handover to its own children carries only the unattended, model, and requirement-closing flags. The plan run identity is derived from date, project, and target, so a re-entry on the same day produces the identical identifier, the emit step skips it as already recorded, and the chain never observes the completion event it waits on; meanwhile the plan and the session directory are reused with every task already marked done, so the re-entry can report completion without executing a single task. Carry-forward has the mirror problem: it is declared the primary response and is recorded as a residual naming the receiving wave, but neither the per-wave authoring step nor the pipeline step is told to read it, so the primary response terminates in a record with no consumer and the carried defect is absent from the receiving wave's denominator and is never re-detected. A wave appended solely to receive carried findings corresponds to no section of the input document, so it cannot be given the source coordinates the design baseline requires and its verification denominators become undefined. The final pass compounds this: it declares reuse of four things and does not include the remediation routing, its remediation rows are all phrased in terms of the wave the finding belongs to although a run-scope finding belongs to none, and its two statements about halting contradict each other — one makes a requirement-level finding an unconditional halt, the other reserves halting for when neither carry-forward path exists. The journal also omits each wave's diff base and head, which the preservation layer, the cross-wave decision, and the fixer scope all presuppose, and the recorded existing-module list has no reader at all.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Pipeline re-entry SHALL accept an explicit unaddressed-requirement scope and an explicit plan run identity, so a verification-driven re-entry does not re-run the whole plan.
- [x] AC-2: A re-entry SHALL be distinguishable from the run it re-enters at the event-emission layer, so the chain observes a fresh completion event instead of an idempotent skip, and a re-entry that executes no task SHALL NOT report completion.
- [x] AC-3: The all-wave final verification SHALL have its own remediation routing for run-scope findings, stating how such a finding reaches an executing wave and that the pass is re-run afterwards.
- [x] AC-4: The rule governing when a cross-wave finding halts the run SHALL be stated consistently in one form — halting reserved for when no carry-forward path exists — and a requirement-level finding SHALL have a stated route rather than only an unconditional halt.
- [x] AC-5: Wave entry SHALL collect every residual carried into that wave and SHALL include it in both the requirement-authoring input and the re-entry scope.
- [x] AC-6: A wave appended solely to receive carried findings SHALL have a defined design-layer denominator derived from the carried findings, so its verification denominators remain computable without a source-document range.
- [x] AC-7: Each wave's diff base and head commit SHALL be recorded on the journal, so the preservation layer, the cross-wave decision, and the fixer scope are reconstructible after a resume without conversation state.
- [x] AC-8: The recorded existing-module list SHALL have a declared consumer at a stated point in the flow rather than being written and never read.
